(7.27)

\displaystyle\begin{split}I^{\prime}={}&\tfrac{1}{4}S_{\textsc{dcr}}+\tfrac{1}{8}({\mathscr{A}}_{\textsc{bhr}}\,\mathchoice{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.36363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25972pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{bhr}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.36363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25972pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{bhr}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.36363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25972pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{bhr}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.36363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25972pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{bhr}}}{0}}}})^{2}\\
&-\tfrac{1}{4}({\mathscr{A}}_{\textsc{bhr}}\mathchoice{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.36363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25972pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{bhr}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.36363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25972pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{bhr}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.36363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25972pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{bhr}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.36363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25972pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{bhr}}}{0}}}})\left[({\mathscr{A}}_{\textsc{i}}\mathchoice{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{i}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{i}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{i}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{i}}}{0}}}})\cos(\psi_{\textsc{bhr}}-\psi_{\textsc{i}})+({\mathscr{A}}_{\textsc{ii}}\mathchoice{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.94443pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{ii}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.94443pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{ii}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.94443pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{ii}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.94443pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{ii}}}{0}}}})\cos(\psi_{\textsc{bhr}}-\psi_{\textsc{ii}})\right]\,,\end{split}

(7.28)

where the difference in sign of the interference terms involving the third ray arises because of the
\pi
phase shift at the beam splitter.
The signal in the
bhr
scheme is then obtained by measuring the difference of the two intensities, i.e.

S_{\textsc{bhr}}=\tfrac{1}{2}({\mathscr{A}}_{\textsc{bhr}}\mathchoice{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.36363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25972pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{bhr}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.36363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25972pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{bhr}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.36363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25972pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{bhr}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.95839pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[7.36363pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[5.25972pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{bhr}}}{0}}}})\left({\mathscr{A}}_{\textsc{i}}\mathchoice{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{i}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{i}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{i}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{i}}}{0}}}}\cos(\psi_{\textsc{bhr}}-\psi_{\textsc{i}})+{\mathscr{A}}_{\textsc{ii}}\mathchoice{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.94443pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{ii}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.94443pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{ii}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.94443pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{ii}}}{0}}}}{K^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.5278pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.72223pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.94443pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.98613pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.45pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.75pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{{\textsc{ii}}}{0}}}}\cos(\psi_{\textsc{bhr}}-\psi_{\textsc{ii}})\right)\,.

(7.29)

The unperturbed signal in this readout scheme is

S_{\textsc{bhr}}^{(0)}=\tfrac{1}{2}\left[\cos(\varphi-2\omega\ell_{\textsc{i}})-\cos(\varphi-2\omega\ell_{\textsc{ii}})\right]\,,

(7.30)

which vanishes for all values of
\varphi
if the arm lengths are chosen to be equal.

As for the
dcr
scheme, it is convenient to separate the first-order perturbations of the phase, amplitude, and frequency by writing

S_{\textsc{bhr}}=S_{\textsc{bhr}}^{(0)}+\varepsilon S_{{\textsc{bhr}},\psi}+\varepsilon S_{{\textsc{bhr}},{\mathscr{A}}}+\varepsilon S_{{\textsc{bhr}},K}+O(\varepsilon^{2})\,,

(7.31)

which, for equal arm lengths, evaluate to

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{{\textsc{bhr}},\psi}={}&+\tfrac{1}{2}\sin(\varphi-2\omega\ell)\\
&\times\bigg(H({\check{k}}_{\textsc{i}},\kappa.x,\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{i}})+H(k_{\textsc{i}},\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{i}},\kappa.x^{E}_{\textsc{i}})\\
&\qquad-H({\check{k}}_{\textsc{ii}},\kappa.x,\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{ii}})-H(k_{\textsc{ii}},\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{ii}},\kappa.x^{E}_{\textsc{ii}})\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(7.32)

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{{\textsc{bhr}},{\mathscr{A}}}={}&-\tfrac{1}{4}\omega_{g}\ell\cos(\varphi-2\omega\ell)\\
&\times\bigg((n.{m_{\textsc{i}}})\,h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})_{\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{i}}}-(1+n.{m_{\textsc{i}}})h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})_{\kappa.x^{E}_{\textsc{i}}}\\
&\qquad-(n.{m_{\textsc{ii}}})\,h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})_{\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{ii}}}+(1+n.{m_{\textsc{ii}}})h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})_{\kappa.x^{E}_{\textsc{ii}}}\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(7.33)

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{{\textsc{bhr}},K}={}&-\tfrac{1}{4}\omega_{g}\omega\cos(\varphi-2\omega\ell)\\
&\times\bigg(\frac{h({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})}{\kappa.{\check{k}}_{\textsc{i}}}\bigg|_{\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{i}}}^{\kappa.x}+\frac{h({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})}{\kappa.k_{\textsc{i}}}\bigg|^{\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{i}}}_{\kappa.x^{E}_{\textsc{i}}}-\frac{h({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})}{\kappa.{\check{k}}_{\textsc{ii}}}\bigg|_{\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{ii}}}^{\kappa.x}-\frac{h({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})}{\kappa.k_{\textsc{ii}}}\bigg|^{\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{ii}}}_{\kappa.x^{E}_{\textsc{ii}}}\bigg)\,,\end{split}

(7.34)

While the phase response in the
bhr
scheme (
7.32
) is the same as in the
dcr
scheme (
7.23
), the amplitude and frequency responses differ. Specifically,
Eqs.

7.24
and
7.25
contain the sum of the perturbations in the two interferometer arms, while
Eqs.

7.33
and
7.34
contain their difference.

8
Detector Pattern Functions in the Low Frequency Limit

In the low frequency approximation, which applies if the
gw
wavelength is much longer than the interferometer arms, the above formulae can be simplified significantly by assuming the
gw
waveform to vary slowly along the light ray trajectories. Formally, this corresponds to a Taylor expansion of
h
near
x=0
to leading order (i.e. including the first non-trivial term).

To set up the notation, we first consider the well-known phase perturbations in
Eqs.

7.23
and
7.32
. To analyse the phase response of the interferometer in both readout schemes, set

S_{\psi}=\tfrac{1}{2}\left[H({\check{k}}_{\textsc{i}},\kappa.x,\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{i}})+H(k_{\textsc{i}},\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{i}},\kappa.x^{E}_{\textsc{i}})-H({\check{k}}_{\textsc{ii}},\kappa.x,\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{ii}})-H(k_{\textsc{ii}},\kappa.x^{R}_{\textsc{ii}},\kappa.x^{E}_{\textsc{ii}})\right]\,,

(8.1)

so that

\displaystyle S_{{\textsc{dcr}},\psi}

\displaystyle=\sin(2\omega\Delta\ell)S_{\psi}\,,

\displaystyle S_{{\textsc{bhr}},\psi}

\displaystyle=\sin(\varphi-2\omega\ell)S_{\psi}\,,

(8.2)

Assuming (almost) equal arm lengths, the low-frequency limit of
S_{\psi}
evaluates to

S_{\psi}\approx\tfrac{1}{2}\omega\ell\left(h({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})-h({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})\right)\,,

(8.3)

where the metric perturbation,
h
, is evaluated at the coordinate origin.
Decomposing
h
in terms of the two polarisations “
+
” and “
\times
” (described in more detail below) as

\mathchoice{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{h^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}(u)=\mathchoice{A^{{{+}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{A^{{{+}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{A^{{{+}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{A^{{{+}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}\,h_{+}(u)+\mathchoice{A^{{{\times}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{A^{{{\times}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{A^{{{\times}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{A^{{{\times}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}\,h_{\times}(u)\,,

(8.4)

one can write

\displaystyle S_{\psi}

\displaystyle\approx\omega\ell\left(F_{\psi}^{+}h_{+}+F_{\psi}^{\times}h_{\times}\right)\,,

(8.5)

where

\displaystyle F_{\psi}^{+}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}[A^{+}({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})-A^{+}({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})]\,,

\displaystyle F_{\psi}^{\times}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}[A^{\times}({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})-A^{\times}({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})]\,.

(8.6)

The functions
F_{\psi}^{+}
and
F_{\psi}^{\times}
are known as the detector pattern functions).
To describe their behaviour for various incidence angles of the gravitational wave, one must parameterise the
gw
wave vector as well as the
gw
polarisation tensors
A^{+}
and
A^{\times}
. Specifically, one must relate the detection coordinate system
xyz
(where the interferometer arms span the
x
and
y
axes) to a suitable coordinate system
XYZ
associated to the gravitational wave.

Figure 2
:
Illustration of the Euler angles
\Phi
,
\Theta
and
\Psi
in the “ZYZ” convention.

For this purpose, it is customary to use Euler angles of the kind “ZYZ”, as depicted in
Fig.

2
. The
gw
coordinate axes
XYZ
are then obtained from the detector axes
xyz
by first rotating by an angle
\Phi
about the
z
-axis, then by an angle
\Theta
about the transformed
y
axis, and finally by an angle
\Psi
about the such transformed
z
axis.
The associated matrix which transforms the
gw
coordinates to the detector coordinates is

R(\Phi,\Theta,\Psi)=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\Phi&-\sin\Phi&0\\
\sin\Phi&\phantom{-}\cos\Phi&0\\
0&\phantom{-}0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\phantom{-}\cos\Theta&0&\sin\Theta\\
0&1&0\\
-\sin\Theta&0&\cos\Theta\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\cos\Psi&-\sin\Psi&0\\
\sin\Psi&\phantom{-}\cos\Psi&0\\
0&\phantom{-}0&1\end{pmatrix}\,.

(8.7)

For comparison, we note that the transformation matrix given in Ref.
[
2
]
uses the “ZXZ” convention for Euler angles and is thus obtained from the one here by the substitution
{\Phi\to\Phi-\pi/2}
,
{\Psi\to\Psi+\pi/2}
.
To parameterise the
gw
polarisation tensors, denote by
\mathfrak{e}_{1},\mathfrak{e}_{2},\mathfrak{e}_{3}
the canonical orthonormal basis of
\mathbf{R}^{3}
.
The
X
and
Y
basis vectors of the
gw
frame then correspond to the following vectors in the detection frame:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{1}

\displaystyle=R(\Phi,\Theta,\Psi)\mathfrak{e}_{1}\,,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{2}

\displaystyle=R(\Phi,\Theta,\Psi)\mathfrak{e}_{2}\,,

(8.8)

using which one can define the polarisation tensors of
Eq.

8.4
as

\displaystyle A^{+}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{b}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{b}_{1}-\mathfrak{b}_{2}\otimes\mathfrak{b}_{2}\,,

\displaystyle A^{\times}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{b}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{b}_{2}+\mathfrak{b}_{2}\otimes\mathfrak{b}_{1}\,.

(8.9)

These quantities depend implicitly on all Euler angles
\Phi,\Theta,\Psi
, but it suffices to consider
\Psi=0
since the general case is obtained from the particular using the formula

\begin{pmatrix}A^{+}(\Phi,\Theta,\Psi)\\
A^{\times}(\Phi,\Theta,\Psi)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\phantom{-}\cos(2\Psi)&\sin(2\Psi)\\
-\sin(2\Psi)&\cos(2\Psi)\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}A^{+}(\Phi,\Theta,0)\\
A^{\times}(\Phi,\Theta,0)\end{pmatrix}\,.

(8.10)

With this parametrisation of the
gw
polarisation, and choosing
{m_{\textsc{i}}}=\mathfrak{e}_{1}
as well as
{m_{\textsc{ii}}}=\mathfrak{e}_{2}
, the pattern functions (
8.6
) evaluate to

\displaystyle F_{\psi}^{+}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}(1+\cos(\Theta)^{2})\cos(2\Phi)\,,

\displaystyle F_{\psi}^{\times}

\displaystyle=-\cos(\Theta)\sin(2\Phi)\,,

(8.11)

in agreement with standard results
[
47
, Eq. (58)]
. The absolute values of these functions are plotted in
Fig.

3
, where it is seen that the
gw
perturbation of the phase is maximal if the
gw
propagates orthogonally to both interferometer arms.

(a)

Eikonal pattern function
|F_{\psi}^{+}|

(b)

Eikonal pattern function
|F_{\psi}^{\times}|

Figure 3
:
Eikonal antenna pattern functions for
\Psi=0
.
The left panel (a) shows the antenna response pattern for a
+
polarised
gw
, the right panel (b) shows the corresponding pattern for a
\times
polarised
gw
.

Let us now consider the terms which are beyond the eikonal equation.

DC Readout Scheme

The first corrections beyond the eikonal equation in the
dcr
scheme are given by
Eqs.

7.24
and
7.25
. Expanding these expressions to first order in the low-frequency limit, one obtains

\displaystyle S_{{\textsc{dcr}},{\mathscr{A}}}

\displaystyle\approx\,\tfrac{1}{2}\omega_{g}\ell\sin^{2}(\omega\Delta\ell)\left(h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})+h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})\right)\,,

(8.12)

\displaystyle S_{{\textsc{dcr}},K}

\displaystyle\approx-\omega_{g}\ell\sin^{2}(\omega\Delta\ell)\left(h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})+h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})\right)\,,

(8.13)

where, as above, the metric perturbation is evaluated at the spatial coordinate origin.
Since the expressions coincide up to an overall factor, the corrections beyond the eikonal equation can be summarised as

\displaystyle\delta S_{\textsc{dcr}}\equiv S_{{\textsc{dcr}},{\mathscr{A}}}+S_{{\textsc{dcr}},K}\approx-\tfrac{1}{2}\omega_{g}\ell\sin^{2}(\omega\Delta\ell)\left(h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})+h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})\right)\,.

(8.14)

Separating the two
gw
polarisations as in
Eq.

8.4
, this can be written as

\delta S_{\textsc{dcr}}\approx\omega_{g}\ell\,\sin^{2}(\omega\Delta\ell)\left[F_{\textsc{dcr}}^{+}h^{\prime}_{+}+F_{\textsc{dcr}}^{\times}h^{\prime}_{\times}\right]\,,

(8.15)

where the antenna pattern functions describing the response beyond the eikonal equation in the
dcr
scheme are

\displaystyle F_{\textsc{dcr}}^{+}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{2}[A^{+}(m_{\textsc{i}},m_{\textsc{i}})+A^{+}(m_{\textsc{ii}},m_{\textsc{ii}})]\,,

\displaystyle F_{\textsc{dcr}}^{\times}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{2}[A^{\times}(m_{\textsc{i}},m_{\textsc{i}})+A^{\times}(m_{\textsc{ii}},m_{\textsc{ii}})]\,.

(8.16)

Setting
{m_{\textsc{i}}}=\mathfrak{e}_{1}
,
{m_{\textsc{ii}}}=\mathfrak{e}_{2}
and inserting the parametrisation of
A^{+}
and
A^{\times}
from
Eq.

8.9
, they evaluate (for
\Psi=0
) to

\displaystyle F^{+}_{\textsc{dcr}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sin^{2}(\Theta)\,,

\displaystyle F^{\times}_{\textsc{dcr}}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(8.17)

These pattern functions coincide (up to the overall factor
1/2
) with those of resonant bar detectors
[
48
, Eq. (8.64)]
. This is because the term in parentheses in
Eq.

8.14
can be written as
-h^{\prime}(\mathfrak{e}_{3},\mathfrak{e}_{3})
since
h
is traceless, and resonant bar detectors are similarly only sensitive to one component of the metric perturbation. However, the expressions here contain
derivatives
of
h
, while mechanical systems directly respond to the strain
h
without differentiation.
Irrespective of this analogy, we note that the amplitude and frequency corrections are sensitive only to
+
polarised
gw
’s (using the convention
\Psi=0
).

The angular dependence of
F^{+}_{\textsc{dcr}}
(in absolute value) is plotted in
Fig.

4(a)
. Clearly, the effect is maximal when the
gw
wave vector lies in the
xy
plane (spanned by the interferometer arms), and a
gw
propagating orthogonally to both arms produces no amplitude perturbations (contrary to the phase perturbation, which is maximal for orthogonal propagation).

Balanced Homodyne Readout Scheme

The analysis of the signals in the
bhr
scheme proceeds analogously to those in the
dcr
scheme.
Expanding
Eqs.

7.33
and
7.34
to leading order in the low-frequency limit, one obtains

\displaystyle S_{{\textsc{bhr}},{\mathscr{A}}}

\displaystyle\approx+\tfrac{1}{4}\omega_{g}\ell\cos(\varphi-2\omega\ell)\left(h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})-h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})\right)\,,

(8.18)

\displaystyle S_{{\textsc{bhr}},K}

\displaystyle\approx-\tfrac{1}{2}\omega_{g}\ell\cos(\varphi-2\omega\ell)\left(h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})-h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})\right)\,.

(8.19)

Also here, the first corrections beyond the eikonal equation can be summarised succinctly as

\displaystyle\delta S_{\textsc{bhr}}\equiv S_{{\textsc{bhr}},{\mathscr{A}}}+S_{{\textsc{bhr}},K}\approx-\tfrac{1}{4}\omega_{g}\ell\cos(\varphi-2\omega\ell)\left(h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{i}}},{m_{\textsc{i}}})-h^{\prime}({m_{\textsc{ii}}},{m_{\textsc{ii}}})\right)\,,

(8.20)

and decomposing the
gw
waveform into its two polarisation components (as above), this can be written as

\delta S_{\textsc{bhr}}\approx\omega_{g}\ell\,\cos(\varphi-2\omega\ell)\left[F_{\textsc{bhr}}^{+}h^{\prime}_{+}+F_{\textsc{bhr}}^{\times}h^{\prime}_{\times}\right]\,,

(8.21)

where the antenna pattern functions for the response beyond the eikonal equation in the
bhr
scheme (for
\Psi=0
) are

\displaystyle F^{+}_{\textsc{bhr}}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{4}(1+\cos(\Theta)^{2})\cos(2\Phi)\,,

\displaystyle F^{\times}_{\textsc{bhr}}

\displaystyle=+\tfrac{1}{2}\cos(\Theta)\sin(2\Phi)\,.

(8.22)

Up to a factor
-1/2
, these functions coincide with the eikonal pattern functions given in
Eq.

8.11
. Their absolute values are plotted in
Figs.

4(b)
and
4(c)
, for direct comparison with the pattern function of the
dcr
scheme.

(a)

Pattern function
F_{\textsc{dcr}}^{+}

(b)

Pattern function
F_{\textsc{bhr}}^{+}

(c)

Pattern function
F_{\textsc{bhr}}^{\times}

Figure 4
:
Absolute values of the antenna pattern functions beyond the eikonal equation.
In the DC readout scheme only
+
polarised
gw
’s contribute (a), while in the balanced homodyne readout scheme both
gw
polarisations produce corrections to the eikonal signal (b, c).

Angular Efficiency Factors

To compare the sensitivity of the two readout schemes, it is customary to define the angular “sky-average” of a function
f
as

\overline{f}:=\frac{1}{4\pi}\int f(\Theta,\Phi)\sin\Theta\,\mathrm{d}\Theta\,\mathrm{d}\Phi\,,

(8.23)

and to define the angular efficiency factor as

\overline{F^{2}}:=\overline{(F^{+})^{2}}+\overline{(F^{\times})^{2}}\,.

(8.24)

For the eikonal response, and the
dcr
and
bhr
responses to corrections beyond the eikonal equation, these factors evaluate to

\displaystyle\overline{F_{\psi}^{2}}

\displaystyle=2/5\,,

\displaystyle\overline{F_{\textsc{dcr}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=2/15\,,

\displaystyle\overline{F_{\textsc{bhr}}^{2}}

\displaystyle=1/10\,.

(8.25)

This shows that the
dcr
scheme is more susceptible to amplitude and frequency perturbations than the
bhr
scheme by a factor of
\sqrt{\overline{F_{\textsc{dcr}}^{2}}/\overline{F_{\textsc{bhr}}^{2}}}=\sqrt{4/3}
.

Waveforms

To illustrate the signals produced by the first corrections beyond the eikonal equation, we plot the detector responses for the
gw
waveform of peak amplitude
\varepsilon\approx 4.75\times 10^{-21}
, shown in
Fig.

5
. This waveform was generated from the
SEOBNRv4
model
[
49
]
using the
PyCBC
software package
[
50
]
.

Figure 5
:
Exemplary waveform of a merger of two black holes of equal mass
20\,\text{M}_{\odot}
at a luminosity distance of
100\,\text{Mpc}
.
This waveform was generated from the
SEOBNRv4
model using the
PyCBC
software package.

The inspiral phase of the waveform shown here starts with a frequency of
\omega_{g}\approx 2\pi\times 40\,\text{Hz}
. This is to be contrasted with typical laser frequencies of
\omega\approx 2\pi\times 280\,\text{THz}
(
ligo
currently uses a laser of
1065\,\text{nm}
wavelength), so that the corrections beyond the eikonal equation are thus suppressed relatively to the phase signal by a factor
\omega_{g}/\omega\approx 1.43\times 10^{-13}
.

Defining the dimensionless functions

\displaystyle f_{\psi}

\displaystyle=F_{\psi}^{+}h_{+}+F_{\psi}^{\times}h_{\times}\,,

\displaystyle f_{\textsc{dcr}}

\displaystyle=F_{\textsc{dcr}}^{+}h^{\prime}_{+}+F_{\textsc{dcr}}^{\times}h^{\prime}_{\times}\,,

\displaystyle f_{\textsc{bhr}}

\displaystyle=F_{\textsc{bhr}}^{+}h^{\prime}_{+}+F_{\textsc{bhr}}^{\times}h^{\prime}_{\times}\,,

(8.26)

the signals at leading order in the low-frequency limit can be written as

\displaystyle S_{\psi,{\textsc{dcr}}}

\displaystyle\approx\omega\ell\,f_{\psi}\sin(2\omega\Delta\ell)\,,

\displaystyle S_{\psi,{\textsc{bhr}}}

\displaystyle\approx\omega\ell\,f_{\psi}\sin(\varphi-2\omega\ell)\,,

(8.27)

\displaystyle\delta S_{{\textsc{dcr}}}

\displaystyle\approx\omega_{g}\ell\,f_{\textsc{dcr}}\sin^{2}(\omega\Delta\ell)\,,

\displaystyle\delta S_{{\textsc{bhr}}}

\displaystyle\approx\omega_{g}\ell\,f_{\textsc{bhr}}\cos(\varphi-2\omega\ell)\,.

(8.28)

Figure

6
shows plots of the functions (
8.26
) for the
gw
waveform depicted in
Fig.

5
. Note that these functions are normalised to a peak value of order unity (left scale), while their contributions to the detected signal differ:
f_{\psi}
is multiplied by
\omega\ell
, while
f_{\textsc{dcr}}
and
f_{\textsc{bhr}}
are multiplied by
\omega_{g}\ell
, so that the latter two are suppressed relatively to the phase signal by
\omega_{g}/\omega
(right scale).

(a)

If the
gw
propagates orthogonally to both interferometer arms, the phase response in both readout schemes and the beyond-eikonal response in the
bhr
scheme are maximal, while the
dcr
scheme is insensitive to beyond-eikonal corrections in this case (not shown).

(b)

If the
gw
propagates parallelly to either of the interferometer arms, both readout schemes are susceptible to corrections beyond the eikonal equation.

Figure 6
:

Comparison of the phase response and first corrections beyond the eikonal equation for the
gw
waveform depicted in
Fig.

5
for normal incidence (a) and parallel incidence (b).
The left scale indicate the values of
f_{\psi}
,
f_{\textsc{dcr}}
and
f_{\textsc{bhr}}
, while the right scale indicates the contributions to the observable signal where
f_{\textsc{dcr}}
and
f_{\textsc{bhr}}
are suppressed relatively to
f_{\psi}
by the frequency ratio
\omega_{g}/\omega\approx 1.43\times 10^{-13}
.

Figure

6(a)
shows the signals produced for the case where the
gw
propagates orthogonally to both interferometer arms. In this case,
S_{\psi}
and
\delta S_{\textsc{bhr}}
are maximal, while
\delta S_{\textsc{dcr}}
vanishes.
For comparison,
Figure

6(b)
shows the signals for the case where the
gw
propagates parallelly to one of the interferometer arms (in which case the phase response is sensitive only to the
+
polarisation mode). In this configuration, both the
dcr
and the
bhr
schemes are susceptible to beyond-eikonal corrections and, in fact, the
dcr
scheme produces a stronger response.

However, since the corrections shown here are suppressed relatively to the phase response by the frequency ratio
\omega_{g}/\omega\approx 10^{-13}
, the experimental detection of such effects in the foreseeable future seems highly unlikely.

9
Discussion

We have determined the response of laser interferometric gravitational wave detectors at one order of accuracy beyond the eikonal equation by solving also the transport equations for the scalar amplitude and the polarisation vector. To this end, we have modelled the emission of monochromatic plane waves, described their propagation and reflection, as well as their behaviour at beam splitters.
The final result for the interferometric output intensity encompasses
gw
-induced perturbations of the optical path length (via the eikonal
\psi
), modulations of the optical frequency (as given by the time-component of the local wave vectors
K
), and scintillations (described by the amplitudes
{\mathscr{A}}
), but was found to be independent of the perturbation of the polarisation vector
E
arising from a non-trivial holonomy integral along the light rays.

The analysis presented here goes beyond previous results which have either allowed for arbitrary
gw
incidence angles but were restricted to the level of the eikonal equation
[
26
]
, or have described amplitude and polarisation effects but were restricted to special alignments
[
25
,
27
,
31
]
. Moreover, the analysis here does not rely on unidimensional approximations such as in Refs.
[
24
,
29
]
, and the result is well-behaved also for small angles between the
gw
and
em
wave vectors, where calculations based on the vector potential often find difficulties
[
22
]
.

Finally, we comment on the model assumptions made here.
The geometry of the interferometer was assumed to be well-described in terms of the background geometry (when using transverse-traceless coordinates), which relies on the assumption that all material points of the interferometer follow geodesic motion. This will not hold exactly true in experiment, and deviations from this model would require detailed descriptions of the elastic properties of the respective materials. However, since the mirrors and beam splitters are much smaller than the interferometer arm lengths, we expect such corrections to be suppressed by the ratio of the object size compared to the arm length and thus negligible for most applications.
Further, we have made specific assumptions of the light sent into the interferometer. While the assumptions on the eikonal and amplitude are simple to interpret (emission occurs with definite and fixed frequency and the emitted radiation has constant energy density), the emitted polarisation is more difficult to model, leading to the unspecified term
E^{\prime}
in
Eq.

4.4
. However, the final result is independent of
E^{\prime}
due to it being orthogonal to the unperturbed polarisation
E^{(0)}
. Hence, we expect most other models to differ mainly in the choice of the emission surfaces on which the boundary conditions are prescribed, rather than in the data prescribed thereon. However, provided the surfaces differ from the ones used here only at first order in
\varepsilon
, such arising corrections can be described entirely using the unperturbed transport equations since corrections to the latter would produce terms quadratic in
\varepsilon
.

We expect that the calculations presented here can be generalised without much complication to Fabry-Pérot cavities, while optical cavities whose mirrors are not plane-parallel (but rather concentric or confocal, for example) could be described using similar techniques, but would require different boundary conditions adapted to the problem.

Appendix A
Derivation of the Polarisation Transport Equations

The equations of geometrical optics can be motivated by writing the electromagnetic field strength tensor as

\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}=\mathchoice{\mathfrak{f}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\mathfrak{f}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\mathfrak{f}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\mathfrak{f}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}e^{i\omega\psi}\,,

(A.1)

and inserting for
\mathchoice{\mathfrak{f}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\mathfrak{f}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\mathfrak{f}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{\mathfrak{f}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}
a
formal
expansion in inverse powers of
(i\omega)
, see e.g. Refs.
[
36
,
37
,
51
]
.
Using
Eq.

A.1
, Maxwell’s equation in vacuum take the form

\displaystyle i\omega\mathrm{d}\psi\wedge\mathfrak{f}+\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{f}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle i\omega\nabla\psi\cdot\mathfrak{f}+\nabla\cdot\mathfrak{f}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(A.2)

where a dot indicates contraction of adjacent indices.
Together, these equations imply

i\omega|\nabla\psi|^{2}\mathfrak{f}+\nabla\psi\cdot\mathfrak{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{f}+\mathrm{d}\psi\wedge(\nabla\cdot\mathfrak{f})=0\,.

(A.3)

Assuming
\psi
to satisfy the eikonal equation

|\nabla\psi|^{2}\equiv\mathchoice{g^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\psi)(\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}\psi)=0\,,

(A.4)

one is left with the intermediate result

\nabla\psi\cdot\mathfrak{\mathrm{d}}\mathfrak{f}+\mathrm{d}\psi\wedge(\nabla\cdot\mathfrak{f})=0\,.

(A.5)

Further, taking the divergence of the first equation in (
A.2
) and adding the exterior derivative of the second yields

\mathrm{d}(\nabla\psi\cdot{\mathfrak{f}})+\nabla\cdot(\mathrm{d}\psi\wedge{\mathfrak{f}})=(i\omega)^{-1}\square_{\text{HdR}}{\mathfrak{f}}\,,

(A.6)

where
\square_{\text{HdR}}{\mathfrak{f}}=-\mathrm{d}(\nabla\cdot{\mathfrak{f}})-\nabla\cdot(\mathrm{d}{\mathfrak{f}})
is the Hodge-de Rham wave operator, which is related to the connection-induced wave operator by the Weitzenböck identity

{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{\nu}{\alpha}}}}{R^{{{\sigma}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}{\nu}{\alpha}}}}\mathchoice{{\mathfrak{f}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\beta}{\sigma}}}}{{\mathfrak{f}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\beta}{\sigma}}}}{{\mathfrak{f}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\beta}{\sigma}}}}{{\mathfrak{f}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\beta}{\sigma}}}}\right)\,,

(A.7)

where
\mathchoice{R^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}{\mu}{\nu}}}}{R^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}{\mu}{\nu}}}}{R^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}{\mu}{\nu}}}}{R^{{{\alpha}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\beta}{\mu}{\nu}}}}
denotes the Riemann curvature tensor.
A direct calculation shows

\mathrm{d}(\nabla\psi\cdot{\mathfrak{f}})+\nabla\cdot(\mathrm{d}\psi\wedge{\mathfrak{f}})=2\nabla_{\nabla\psi}{\mathfrak{f}}+(\square\psi){\mathfrak{f}}-\left(\mathrm{d}\psi\wedge(\nabla\cdot{\mathfrak{f}})+(\nabla\psi)\cdot\mathrm{d}{\mathfrak{f}}\right)\,,

(A.8)

where the last term vanishes by virtue of
Eq.

A.5
.

Equation

A.6
thus yields

2\nabla_{\nabla\psi}{\mathfrak{f}}+(\square\psi){\mathfrak{f}}=(i\omega)^{-1}\square_{\text{HdR}}{\mathfrak{f}}\,,

(A.9)

from which the transport equations of geometrical optics can be obtained by inserting the formal expansion

{\mathfrak{f}}=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}(i\omega)^{-m}\,{\mathfrak{f}}_{(m)}\,,

(A.10)

which yields

\displaystyle 2\nabla_{\nabla\psi}{\mathfrak{f}}_{(0)}+(\square\psi){\mathfrak{f}}_{(0)}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle 2\nabla_{\nabla\psi}{\mathfrak{f}}_{(m+1)}+(\square\psi){\mathfrak{f}}_{(m+1)}

\displaystyle=\square_{\text{HdR}}{\mathfrak{f}}_{(m)}\,.

(A.11)

Substituting (
A.10
) in the first-order equations (
A.2
) then yields the algebraic recursion relations

\displaystyle\nabla\psi\cdot{\mathfrak{f}}_{(0)}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\nabla\psi\cdot{\mathfrak{f}}_{(m+1)}

\displaystyle=\nabla\cdot{\mathfrak{f}}_{(m)}\,,

(A.12)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\psi\wedge{\mathfrak{f}}_{(0)}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\psi\wedge{\mathfrak{f}}_{(m+1)}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}{\mathfrak{f}}_{(m)}\,,

(A.13)

which can be thought of as constraint equations limiting the number of degrees of freedom to two.
The geometrical optic scheme described here is in accord with the one given in Ref.
[
52
]
.

We emphasise that the series (
3.5
) is first and foremost a
formal
series, which is not necessarily convergent, but rather asymptotic in many typical applications, see e.g. Ref.
[
37
, Sect. 2.7]
for a discussion of the asymptotic nature of such series for initial data prescribed on spacelike hypersurfaces.
In the concrete setup here, our analysis of the full Einstein-Maxwell equations (in preparation) will assess the quality of the leading order geometrical optics approximation and estimate the errors made in using this approximation.

Appendix B
Interpretation of the Polarisation Vector

In this section, we show how the one-form
E
in the field strength tensor

F={\mathscr{A}}e^{i\omega\psi}(\mathrm{d}\psi)\wedge E

(B.1)

is related to the polarisation of the electromagnetic wave.
Here,
E
is actually only defined up to addition of a multiple of
\mathrm{d}\psi
.

Consider an observer
{\mathscr{O}}
with four-velocity
u
(i.e. a unit timelike vector).
Since the eikonal
\psi
describes the rapidly oscillating phase of the electromagnetic wave, the measured frequency is

\omega_{\mathscr{O}}=-\omega\,\mathchoice{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\psi\,,

(B.2)

so that
-\mathchoice{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\psi=\omega_{\mathscr{O}}/\omega
.
Regarding the electric field
\mathbf{E}
and the magnetic field
\mathbf{B}
as one- and two-forms, respectively, the decomposition of the field strength can be written as

\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}{\nu}}}}=2\mathchoice{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.36111pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[0.97221pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{[}{\mu}}}}{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.2639pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1. (B.3)

where
\mathbf{E}
and
\mathbf{B}
are such that their contractions with
u
vanish (i.e. they are spatial for
{\mathscr{O}}
). In particular, they are determined by

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathbf{E}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathbf{E}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathbf{E}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathbf{E}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}

\displaystyle=-\mathchoice{u^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}{\mu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}{\mu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}{\mu}}}}{F^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\mak (B.4)

We now apply this decomposition to the field given in

where

(B.6)

which is chosen such that
\mathchoice{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{{\bar{E}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{{\bar{E}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{{\bar{E}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{{\bar{E}}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}=0
. The above formula for
\mathbf{E}
then yields

\mathbf{E}=({\mathscr{A}}\omega_{\mathscr{O}}/\omega)\,\bar{E}e^{i\psi}\,,

(B.7)

so that
\bar{E}
determines the direction of the electric field and
{\mathscr{A}}\omega_{\mathscr{O}}/\omega
its amplitude (note that
\bar{E}
has the same norm as
E
). To obtain an expression for the magnetic field
\mathbf{B}
, we first decompose the eikonal gradient as

\mathchoice{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\nabla^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}\psi=\tfrac{\omega_{\mathscr{O}}}{\omega}(\mathchoice{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}+\mathchoice{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c] (B.8)

where
\mathchoice{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}
is a unit spacelike vector orthogonal to
u
.
Using the second equation in (
B.4
), one finds

\mathbf{B}=n\wedge\mathbf{E}\,,

(B.9)

which is the standard formula stating that
\mathbf{E},\mathbf{B}
and
n
form a right-handed orthogonal system, and that the electric and magnetic fields have the same norm.

Appendix C
Constraint Equations for Boundary Data

As is well-known from the
3+1
decomposition of Maxwell’s equations, one is not free to prescribe the electromagnetic field on spacelike hypersurfaces arbitrarily.
Instead, the initially prescribed electric and magnetic fields
\mathbf{E}
and
\mathbf{B}
(in Heaviside–Lorentz units) must satisfy
\mathscr{D}\cdot\mathbf{E}=\rho
and
\mathscr{D}\cdot\mathbf{B}=0
, where
\rho
is the charge density on the initial hypersurface, and
\mathscr{D}
is the spatial covariant derivative.

Similarly, we show here that boundary values prescribed on a
timelike
hypersurface
\Sigma
must also satisfy certain constraint equations, which we compute explicitly for the case of normal emission, as considered in the main body of the text.

To this end, consider a hypersurface-orthogonal unit timelike vector field
u
. With respect to this field, one may decompose the field strength,
F
, as well as its Hodge dual,
\ast F
, in terms electric and magnetic fields as

\displaystyle\mathchoice{F^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{F^{{{\mu}{\nu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(C.3)

where
\mathfrak{L}_{u}
denotes the Lie derivative along the vector field
u
.

Consider now a timelike hypersurface
\Sigma
with unit normal
n
, such that
u
and
n
are orthogonal. This entails that
n
is spatial with respect to
u
, and also that
u
is tangent to
\Sigma
. Contracting
Eqs.

C.2
and
C.3
with the surface normal
n
and imposing normal emission in the sense

(C.6)

where
a=\nabla_{u}u
is the acceleration vector. These equations are constraints on boundary data, as they only involve tangential derivatives, but no derivatives normal to
\Sigma
.

It turns out that the first terms involving
\mathfrak{L}_{u}
of
\mathbf{E}
and
\mathbf{B}
give no contribution.
Indeed, writing the Lie derivative in terms of the Lie bracket
\mathfrak{L}_{u}\mathbf{E}=[u,E]
, and noting that
u
and
E
being tangent to
\Sigma
implies that their Lie bracket is also tangent to
\Sigma
, one finds
\mathchoice{\mathfrak{L}_{u}\mathbf{E}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{L}_{u}\mathbf{E}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{L}_{u}\mathbf{E}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{L}_{u}\mathbf{E}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{n^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}=\mathchoice{\mathfrak{L}_{u}\mathbf{B}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathfrak{L}_{u}\mathbf{B}^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\m .

(C.7)

which are the same for both
\mathbf{E}
and
\mathbf{B}

applies, one may eliminate one of the fields from the constraints.

C.7
) takes the form

(\mathchoice{\delta^{{{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{\delta^{{{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{\delta^{{{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{\delta^{{{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}}}}+\mathchoice{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}-\mathchoice{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\te extstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathbf{E}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}{\mathbf{E}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\mu}}}}=0\,.

(C.9)

Writing

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}E

\displaystyle=(\mathchoice{\delta^{{{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{\delta^{{{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{\delta^{{{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{\delta^{{{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}}}}+\mathchoice{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{u^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathchoice{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}{u^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{\nu}}}}-\mathchoice{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{n^{{{\mu}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86
